For EACH claimed result, check independently:
 (a) CORRECTNESS: reconstruct the argument yourself. Test the key identities/claims symbolically or numerically over a real parameter range. If the expert states a chain of reasoning, check every load-bearing step; a plausible-sounding step that is unproved counts as NOT verified. Actively search for a counterexample.

 (c) NOT-ALREADY-PUBLISHED: query the arXiv API and standard sources. We may cite others' results but must never re-derive a published theorem as ours.
Assign each claim exactly one verdict: CORRECT-AND-NEW / UNVERIFIED (plausible but a step is unproved — say which) / WRONG (give the disproof or counterexample) / ALREADY-IN-PAPER / ALREADY-PUBLISHED (give the citation).

STEP 2 — ACT ON THE VERDICTS:
 - CORRECT-AND-NEW: prove rigorously and integrate into the manuscript with explicit hypotheses, no overclaim; extend the artifacts/ verification script to cover it and run it.
 - UNVERIFIED: do NOT integrate as a theorem. Either close the gap yourself and then integrate, or record it as an explicit open interface.
 - WRONG / ALREADY-*: do not integrate; state the disproof or citation plainly.
 Recompile (latexmk -pdfxe -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error main.tex, or xelatex if that is the paper's engine).

STEP 3 — DESIGN THE NEXT ROUND:
Write the next-round follow-up to the file next_A8.txt in this paper's directory. It must be in Chinese, formal academic register, and must do whichever applies:
 - CHALLENGE any WRONG claim by stating the counterexample and demanding a corrected statement;
 - PRESS any UNVERIFIED claim on exactly the unproved step, demanding a complete proof of that step;
 - If claims were ALREADY-IN-PAPER/PUBLISHED, say so and redirect the expert away from that ground;
 - Then pose the single sharpest genuinely-open question this paper now faces, demanding a deep publishable answer rather than incremental restatement.
End the file with: 不要重复之前内容.不要重复其他人已经发表公开的推理过程(定理、推论、猜想、命题及证明), 可以使用其他人的结论并引用.不要中间过程结论.请使用学术化语言表达, 禁止口语.

Report ONLY: (i) per-claim verdict with the evidence that justifies it; (ii) what you integrated (theorem labels) and what you refused and why; (iii) verification-script result; (iv) compile result; (v) confirm next_A8.txt was written.

# PDF Skill

## When to use
- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Workflow
1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` if available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` (or `pypdf`) for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on it for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Temp and output conventions
- Use `tmp/pdfs/` for intermediate files; delete when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/pdf/` when working in this repo.
- Keep filenames stable and descriptive.

## Dependencies (install if missing)
Prefer `uv` for dependency management.

Python packages:
```
uv pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```
If `uv` is unavailable:
```
python3 -m pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```
System tools (for rendering):
```
# macOS (Homebrew)
brew install poppler

# Ubuntu/Debian
sudo apt-get install -y poppler-utils
```

If installation isn't possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it locally.

## Environment
No required environment variables.

## Rendering command
```
pdftoppm -png $INPUT_PDF $OUTPUT_PREFIX
```

## Quality expectations
- Maintain polished visual design: consistent typography, spacing, margins, and section hierarchy.
- Avoid rendering issues: clipped text, overlapping elements, broken tables, black squares, or unreadable glyphs.
- Charts, tables, and images must be sharp, aligned, and clearly labeled.
- Use ASCII hyphens only. Avoid U+2011 (non-breaking hyphen) and other Unicode dashes.
- Citations and references must be human-readable; never leave tool tokens or placeholder strings.

## Final checks
- Do not deliver until the latest PNG inspection shows zero visual or formatting defects.
- Confirm headers/footers, page numbering, and section transitions look polished.
- Keep intermediate files organized or remove them after final approval.

name: verification-before-completion
description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always
---

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

Claiming work is complete without verification is dishonesty, not efficiency.

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence â‰  evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter â‰  compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion â‰  excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
âœ… [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
âŒ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
âœ… Write â†’ Run (pass) â†’ Revert fix â†’ Run (MUST FAIL) â†’ Restore â†’ Run (pass)
âŒ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
âœ… [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
âŒ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
âœ… Re-read plan â†’ Create checklist â†’ Verify each â†’ Report gaps or completion
âŒ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
âœ… Agent reports success â†’ Check VCS diff â†’ Verify changes â†’ Report actual state
âŒ Trust agent report
```

## Why This Matters

From 24 failure memories:
- your human partner said "I don't believe you" - trust broken
- Undefined functions shipped - would crash
- Missing requirements shipped - incomplete features
- Time wasted on false completion â†’ redirect â†’ rework
- Violates: "Honesty is a core value. If you lie, you'll be replaced."

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

## The Bottom Line

**No shortcuts for verification.**

Run the command. Read the output. THEN claim the result.

This is non-negotiable.

<SUBAGENT-STOP>
If you were dispatched as a subagent to execute a specific task, skip this skill.
</SUBAGENT-STOP>

<EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST invoke the skill.

IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.

This is not negotiable. This is not optional. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>

## Instruction Priority

Superpowers skills override default system prompt behavior, but **user instructions always take precedence**:

1. **User's explicit instructions** (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, AGENTS.md, direct requests) â€” highest priority
2. **Superpowers skills** â€” override default system behavior where they conflict
3. **Default system prompt** â€” lowest priority

If CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, or AGENTS.md says "don't use TDD" and a skill says "always use TDD," follow the user's instructions. The user is in control.

## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to youâ€”follow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

## Platform Adaptation

Skills use Claude Code tool names. Non-CC platforms: see `references/copilot-tools.md` (Copilot CLI), `references/codex-tools.md` (Codex) for tool equivalents. Gemini CLI users get the tool mapping loaded automatically via GEMINI.md.

# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

```dot
digraph skill_flow {
    "User message received" [shape=doublecircle];
    "About to EnterPlanMode?" [shape=doublecircle];
    "Already brainstormed?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" [shape=box];
    "Might any skill apply?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke Skill tool" [shape=box];
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" [shape=box];
    "Has checklist?" [shape=diamond];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [shape=box];
    "Follow skill exactly" [shape=box];
    "Respond (including clarifications)" [shape=doublecircle];

    "About to EnterPlanMode?" -> "Already brainstormed?";
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Invoke brainstorming skill" [label="no"];
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Might any skill apply?" [label="yes"];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" -> "Might any skill apply?";

    "User message received" -> "Might any skill apply?";
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Invoke Skill tool" [label="yes, even 1%"];
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Respond (including clarifications)" [label="definitely not"];
    "Invoke Skill tool" -> "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'";
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" -> "Has checklist?";
    "Has checklist?" -> "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [label="yes"];
    "Has checklist?" -> "Follow skill exactly" [label="no"];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" -> "Follow skill exactly";
}
```

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOPâ€”you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept â‰  using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" â†’ brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" â†’ debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.

name: writing-plans
description: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code

# Writing Plans

## Overview

Write comprehensive implementation plans assuming the engineer has zero context for our codebase and questionable taste. Document everything they need to know: which files to touch for each task, code, testing, docs they might need to check, how to test it. Give them the whole plan as bite-sized tasks. DRY. YAGNI. TDD. Frequent commits.

Assume they are a skilled developer, but know almost nothing about our toolset or problem domain. Assume they don't know good test design very well.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the writing-plans skill to create the implementation plan."

**Context:** This should be run in a dedicated worktree (created by brainstorming skill).

**Save plans to:** `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`
- (User preferences for plan location override this default)

## Scope Check

If the spec covers multiple independent subsystems, it should have been broken into sub-project specs during brainstorming. If it wasn't, suggest breaking this into separate plans â€” one per subsystem. Each plan should produce working, testable software on its own.

## File Structure

Before defining tasks, map out which files will be created or modified and what each one is responsible for. This is where decomposition decisions get locked in.

- Design units with clear boundaries and well-defined interfaces. Each file should have one clear responsibility.
- You reason best about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. Prefer smaller, focused files over large ones that do too much.
- Files that change together should live together. Split by responsibility, not by technical layer.
- In existing codebases, follow established patterns. If the codebase uses large files, don't unilaterally restructure - but if a file you're modifying has grown unwieldy, including a split in the plan is reasonable.

This structure informs the task decomposition. Each task should produce self-contained changes that make sense independently.

## Bite-Sized Task Granularity

**Each step is one action (2-5 minutes):**
- "Write the failing test" - step
- "Run it to make sure it fails" - step
- "Implement the minimal code to make the test pass" - step
- "Run the tests and make sure they pass" - step
- "Commit" - step

## Plan Document Header

**Every plan MUST start with this header:**

```markdown
# [Feature Name] Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** [One sentence describing what this builds]

**Architecture:** [2-3 sentences about approach]

**Tech Stack:** [Key technologies/libraries]

---
```

## Task Structure

````markdown
### Task N: [Component Name]

**Files:**
- Create: `exact/path/to/file.py`
- Modify: `exact/path/to/existing.py:123-145`
- Test: `tests/exact/path/to/test.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
def test_specific_behavior():
    result = function(input)
    assert result == expected
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run test to verify it fails**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: FAIL with "function not defined"

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

```python
def function(input):
    return expected
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: PASS

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

```bash
git add tests/path/test.py src/path/file.py
git commit -m "feat: add specific feature"
```
````

## No Placeholders

Every step must contain the actual content an engineer needs. These are **plan failures** â€” never write them:
- "TBD", "TODO", "implement later", "fill in details"
- "Add appropriate error handling" / "add validation" / "handle edge cases"
- "Write tests for the above" (without actual test code)
- "Similar to Task N" (repeat the code â€” the engineer may be reading tasks out of order)
- Steps that describe what to do without showing how (code blocks required for code steps)
- References to types, functions, or methods not defined in any task

## Remember
- Exact file paths always
- Complete code in every step â€” if a step changes code, show the code
- Exact commands with expected output
- DRY, YAGNI, TDD, frequent commits

## Self-Review

After writing the complete plan, look at the spec with fresh eyes and check the plan against it. This is a checklist you run yourself â€” not a subagent dispatch.

**1. Spec coverage:** Skim each section/requirement in the spec. Can you point to a task that implements it? List any gaps.

**2. Placeholder scan:** Search your plan for red flags â€” any of the patterns from the "No Placeholders" section above. Fix them.

**3. Type consistency:** Do the types, method signatures, and property names you used in later tasks match what you defined in earlier tasks? A function called `clearLayers()` in Task 3 but `clearFullLayers()` in Task 7 is a bug.

If you find issues, fix them inline. No need to re-review â€” just fix and move on. If you find a spec requirement with no task, add the task.

## Execution Handoff

After saving the plan, offer execution choice:

**"Plan complete and saved to `docs/superpowers/plans/<filename>.md`. Two execution options:**

**1. Subagent-Driven (recommended)** - I dispatch a fresh subagent per task, review between tasks, fast iteration

**2. Inline Execution** - Execute tasks in this session using executing-plans, batch execution with checkpoints

**Which approach?"**

**If Subagent-Driven chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development
- Fresh subagent per task + two-stage review

**If Inline Execution chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:executing-plans
- Batch execution with checkpoints for review

以下结论均建立在  的 Assumption 1.1 上。论文已经陈述的 Theorems A–E、商逆公式、串联系统 Prony 可识别性以及局部  $(n-1)^2$  维相似轨道不再复述。此前关于商覆盖、Artin 代入失效、调和正规化及固定标签反演的结论亦已排除。
一、三重包含坐标的精确物理像：一个此前缺失的余维一方程
令  $r=(r_0,r_1,r_2)$  为论文 Theorem B 的三个可见包含概率，并定义

$$a(r)=\frac{r_1}{r_0},\qquad
\lambda(r)=\frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2},$$


$$\sigma_1(r)=1-a(r)+\lambda(r),\qquad
\sigma_2(r)=r_0\{1-\lambda(r)\}-a(r)+\lambda(r).$$

设  $p,s$  为

$$z^2-\sigma_1(r)z+\sigma_2(r)=0$$

的两个根。定义对称对数除差

$$\mathfrak C(p,s)=
\begin{cases}
\dfrac{s\log p-p\log s}{\log p-\log s},&p\neq s,\\[1.2ex]
p(1-\log p),&p=s.
\end{cases}$$

定理 1（精确物理像判别）
在  $r_0>0$ 、 $r_1-r_0^2\neq0$  且  $p,s\in(0,1)$  的自然逆域内， $r$  来源于 Assumption 1.1 的某一组正参数  $(\Gamma,\kappa_r,\Delta\tau)$ ，当且仅当

$$\boxed{\;
\mathcal E(r):=
\frac{r_1}{r_0}-1+\mathfrak C(p,s)=0.
\;}
\tag{1}$$

因此，物理 sampled-counter 子族在一般三维包含坐标空间中不是开集，而是一张精确的余维一解析曲面。
证明
置  $x=\Gamma\Delta\tau$ 、 $y=\kappa_r\Delta\tau$ ，故  $p=e^{-x}$ 、 $s=e^{-y}$ 。积分项满足

$$b=\frac{y(p-s)}{y-x},$$

从而

$$s+b=\frac{yp-xs}{y-x}
     =\frac{s\log p-p\log s}{\log p-\log s}
     =\mathfrak C(p,s).$$

由于  $a=1-s-b$ ，立即得到  $\mathcal E(r)=0$ 。
反之，若 (1) 成立，令

$$b=\mathfrak C(p,s)-s,\qquad a=1-\mathfrak C(p,s).$$

则  $b=y(p-s)/(y-x)$ 。由  $\sigma_1=p+s$  得

$$\lambda=p+s+a-1=p-b.$$

再由  $\sigma_2=ps$  得

$$r_0
 =\frac{ps+a-\lambda}{1-\lambda}
 =\frac{(1-p)(1-s)}{1-p+b},$$

这正是论文中的平稳点击率  $\rho$ 。于是  $r_1=r_0a$ ，且由  $\lambda$  的定义恢复  $r_2$ 。故整个三重坐标恰由该 sampled-counter 参数产生。证毕。
推论 1.1（对角线上的正则模型诊断）
虽然单根  $p,s$  在  $p=s$  处不可微，但  $\mathfrak C$  是对称解析函数。令

$$p=m+d,\qquad s=m-d,$$

则

$$\mathfrak C(m+d,m-d)
=
m(1-\log m)
+
\frac{d^2}{m}
\left(\frac12+\frac13\log m\right)
+O(d^4).
\tag{2}$$

由于  $m=\sigma_1/2$  且  $d^2=\sigma_1^2/4-\sigma_2$ ， $\mathcal E(r)$  在交换对角线上仍为通常可微函数。故 Theorem C 立即给出

$$\sqrt N\,\mathcal E(\widehat r_N^{\rm cyc})
\Longrightarrow
N(0,\nu_{\mathcal E}^2),
\qquad
\nu_{\mathcal E}^2
=
\nabla\mathcal E(r)^\top
\Sigma_r
\nabla\mathcal E(r).
\tag{3}$$

若  $\nu_{\mathcal E}^2>0$ ，则

$$\frac{N\,\mathcal E(\widehat r_N^{\rm cyc})^2}
     {\widehat\nu_{\mathcal E,N}^{\,2}}
\Longrightarrow \chi_1^2.
\tag{4}$$

这给出一个跨越交换对角线仍然正则的、单自由度的物理像失配检验。其逻辑含义仅是检验三重包含坐标是否落在 sampled-counter 像上；不拒绝不能证明完整时间序列模型正确。
一般 DMAP(2) 规范形与矩匹配文献处理更宽的表示类，但未给出上述 sampled-clock 对数除差像方程；参见 Mészáros–Telek 的 DMAP(2) 规范表示。Zamparo 的工作建立一般平稳更新二元序列及其极限定理，也未包含该物理像约束，Zamparo 2022。

二、隐藏模的尖锐谱界与完整相关相图
定理 2（割线谱公式）
令

$$f(t)=te^{-t},\qquad x=\Gamma\Delta\tau,\qquad y=\kappa_r\Delta\tau.$$

则论文中的非平凡隐藏特征值具有精确表示

$$\boxed{\;
\lambda_{\rm hid}
=
\frac{f(y)-f(x)}{y-x},
\;}
\tag{5}$$

在  $x=y$  时按连续延拓解释为

$$\lambda_{\rm hid}=f'(x)=e^{-x}(1-x).
\tag{6}$$

由此得到尖锐全局谱界

$$\boxed{\;
-e^{-2}\leq \lambda_{\rm hid}<1.
\;}
\tag{7}$$

下界仅在  $x=y=2$  处达到；上确界  $1$  仅在  $x,y\downarrow0$  时逼近。
证明
由

$$b=\frac{y(e^{-x}-e^{-y})}{y-x}$$

直接得到

$$e^{-x}-b
=
\frac{ye^{-y}-xe^{-x}}{y-x}.$$

这即为 (5)。由中值定理， $\lambda_{\rm hid}=f'(\xi)$ ，其中  $\xi$  位于  $x,y$  之间。函数

$$f'(t)=e^{-t}(1-t)$$

在  $t=2$  取得唯一最小值  $-e^{-2}$ ，并在  $t\downarrow0$  时趋于  $1$ ，从而得到 (7)。证毕。
推论 2.1（完整相关符号相图）
由于论文已经证明

$$\gamma_n:=\operatorname{Cov}(A_0,A_n)
=
\rho(a-\rho)\lambda_{\rm hid}^{\,n-1},
\qquad
\rho(a-\rho)<0,$$

故：

$$\begin{array}{c|c}
\lambda_{\rm hid}>0 & \gamma_n<0\ \text{对所有 }n\ge1,\\
\lambda_{\rm hid}=0 & \gamma_1<0,\ \gamma_n=0\ \text{对所有 }n\ge2,\\
\lambda_{\rm hid}<0 & \gamma_n\text{严格交替变号}.
\end{array}
\tag{8}$$

尤其在振荡区间中，

$$\left|\frac{\gamma_{n+1}}{\gamma_n}\right|
=
|\lambda_{\rm hid}|
\le e^{-2}\approx0.135335.
\tag{9}$$

因此，负隐藏模所造成的交替相关具有一个该物理子族特有的、远强于一般 D-MAP 谱界的普适衰减常数。
推论 2.2（精确 1-依赖曲线及 Lambert  $W$  参数化）
对于  $0<x\le y$ ，精确 1-依赖当且仅当

$$xe^{-x}=ye^{-y}.
\tag{10}$$

除固定点  $(1,1)$  外，该曲线必跨越  $t=1$ 。若  $0<x<1$ ，唯一对应点为

$$\boxed{\;
y=\tau(x):=-W_{-1}(-xe^{-x})>1.
\;}
\tag{11}$$

该映射为交换  $(0,1)$  与  $(1,\infty)$  的序反转对合。其端点渐近为

$$\tau(x)
=
\log\frac1x+\log\log\frac1x+o(1),
\qquad x\downarrow0,
\tag{12}$$

以及

$$\tau(1-u)
=
1+u+\frac23u^2+\frac49u^3+O(u^4),
\qquad u\downarrow0.
\tag{13}$$

因此，论文所谓的“exact 1-dependence threshold”实际上是一条具有显式全局几何、尖点分支结构和双尺度渐近的解析相界，而非单一未解析的标量条件。

三、交换对角线上的  $N^{-1/4}$  根分裂定律
论文 Theorem E 仅给出对称坐标投影的  $\sqrt N$  极限，没有给出重复根本身的极限。
定理 3（投影后重复根的二尺度极限）
令一般对角点为

$$\sigma_0=(2z,z^2),\qquad 0<z<1,$$

并假设

$$\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}-\sigma_0)
\Longrightarrow
Z=(Z_1,Z_2)^\top
\sim N(0,\Sigma_\sigma(z)).$$

令  $\widehat\sigma_N=\Pi_F(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw})$ ，其中

$$F=\{(\sigma_1,\sigma_2):\sigma_2\le \sigma_1^2/4\}.$$

定义

$$V=zZ_1-Z_2.$$

若  $\widehat z_{-,N}\le\widehat z_{+,N}$  为投影后多项式的两个实根，则

$$\boxed{\;
N^{1/4}
\begin{pmatrix}
\widehat z_{-,N}-z\\[1mm]
\widehat z_{+,N}-z
\end{pmatrix}
\Longrightarrow
\begin{pmatrix}
-\sqrt{V_+}\\[1mm]
\sqrt{V_+}
\end{pmatrix},
\qquad V_+=\max(V,0).
\;}
\tag{14}$$

等价地，

$$N^{1/4}(\widehat z_{+,N}-\widehat z_{-,N})
\Longrightarrow
2\sqrt{V_+}.
\tag{15}$$

若  $\Delta\tau$  已知，令  $\theta_0=-\log z/\Delta\tau$ ，则根排序率满足

$$N^{1/4}
\begin{pmatrix}
-\log\widehat z_{-,N}/\Delta\tau-\theta_0\\
-\log\widehat z_{+,N}/\Delta\tau-\theta_0
\end{pmatrix}
\Longrightarrow
\frac{\sqrt{V_+}}{\Delta\tau\,z}
\begin{pmatrix}1\\-1\end{pmatrix}.
\tag{16}$$

证明
Theorem E 给出

$$X_N=\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_0)
\Longrightarrow
X=\Pi_{H_z}Z,
\qquad
H_z=\{h:h_2\le zh_1\}.$$

欧氏半空间投影满足

$$zX_1-X_2=(zZ_1-Z_2)_+=V_+.
\tag{17}$$

对判别式

$$\Delta(\sigma)=\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2$$

作一阶展开：

$$\sqrt N\,\Delta(\widehat\sigma_N)
=
4(zX_{N,1}-X_{N,2})+o_P(1)
\Longrightarrow4V_+.$$

根的半间距为  $\sqrt{\Delta}/2$ ，因而具有  $N^{-1/4}$  尺度，根中点的  $N^{-1/2}$  扰动在该尺度下消失，由此得到 (14)–(15)。对  $-\log z/\Delta\tau$  作一阶展开即得 (16)。证毕。
在论文唯一认证点  $z=1/2$ ，

$$V=\frac12Z_1-Z_2,\qquad
\operatorname{Var}(V)
=
(1/2,-1)\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)(1/2,-1)^\top
\approx0.932336360.
\tag{18}$$

因此  $V$  为非退化中心正态变量， $V_+$  以概率  $1/2$  等于零。于是投影后两个估计根以渐近概率  $1/2$  完全粘合，其余概率下按平方根正态分裂。这一原子—连续混合分布不能由普通 delta method 得到。
此外，局部备择

$$p_N=z-hN^{-1/4},\qquad s_N=z+hN^{-1/4}$$

满足

$$(p_N+s_N,p_Ns_N)
=
(2z,z^2-h^2N^{-1/2}),
\tag{19}$$

说明根间距只能通过其平方进入  $\sqrt N$  级可见坐标；故  $N^{-1/4}$  并非投影算法的偶然缺陷，而是交换商几何的二阶识别尺度。
一般约束投影极限属于既有理论，Shapiro 2000；近碰撞 Prony 节点的病态性亦已有数值分析研究，Batenkov 2014。但上述 sampled-counter 协方差驱动的  $N^{-1/4}$  原子—平方根正态极限并非这些一般结果的现成陈述。

四、路径级离散化等价与普适半采样区间偏差
定理 4（舍入的二阶段指数等待时间表示）
在 Palm 点击时刻之后，取相互独立的

$$R\sim\operatorname{Exp}(\kappa_r),\qquad
E\sim\operatorname{Exp}(\Gamma),
\qquad W=R+E.$$

则论文中的零串长度满足路径分布恒等式

$$\boxed{\;
G\overset d=\left\lfloor\frac{W}{\Delta\tau}\right\rfloor,
\qquad
G+1\overset d=\left\lceil\frac{W}{\Delta\tau}\right\rceil.
\;}
\tag{20}$$

这不仅重新解释了  $\Gamma\leftrightarrow\kappa_r$  的不可标记对称性，而且给出概率母函数

$$\mathbb E z^G
=
\frac{
\kappa_r(1-p)/(1-pz)
-
\Gamma(1-s)/(1-sz)
}{
\kappa_r-\Gamma
},
\tag{21}$$

以及精确平均周期

$$\mu_\Delta=\mathbb E(G+1)
=
\frac{
y/(1-e^{-x})-x/(1-e^{-y})
}{
y-x
}.
\tag{22}$$

当  $\Delta=\Delta\tau\downarrow0$  而  $\Gamma,\kappa_r$  固定时，

$$\boxed{\;
\Delta\mu_\Delta
=
\frac1\Gamma+\frac1{\kappa_r}
+\frac{\Delta}{2}
+\frac{\Gamma\kappa_r(\Gamma+\kappa_r)}{720}\Delta^4
+O(\Delta^6).
\;}
\tag{23}$$

因此单位时间的可见点击强度满足

$$\frac{\rho}{\Delta}
=
\frac{\Gamma\kappa_r}{\Gamma+\kappa_r}
-
\frac{\Delta}{2}
\left(\frac{\Gamma\kappa_r}{\Gamma+\kappa_r}\right)^2
+O(\Delta^2).
\tag{24}$$

式 (23) 中的  $\Delta/2$  是一个与两种速率无关的普适半区间延迟；式 (24) 则给出边界锁存采样导致的首阶强度下偏差。该项在论文的固定  $\Delta\tau$  分析中没有出现，但对跨采样频率比较和实验设计具有直接意义。

五、高维最小纤维不是“包含一条轨道”，而是完整的全局主轨道
令

$$C(K)=[\,\mathbf1,K\mathbf1,\ldots,K^{n-1}\mathbf1\,],$$

并令  $K$  为论文 Proposition 3.1 意义下的最小内点。定义

$$\Omega_K=
\left\{
M\in G_n:
M^{-1}KM>0,\ 
(I-M^{-1}KM)\mathbf1>0
\right\}.$$

定理 5（全局纤维参数化）
最小内点  $K$  的完整可见纤维恰为

$$\boxed{\;
\mathcal F^\circ(K)
=
\{M^{-1}KM:M\in\Omega_K\},
\;}
\tag{25}$$

且轨道参数  $M$  是唯一的。若  $L=M^{-1}KM$ ，则其显式逆为

$$\boxed{\;
M=C(K)\,C(L)^{-1}.
\;}
\tag{26}$$

因此轨道映射不是仅在单位元附近的浸入，而是从开放半代数集  $\Omega_K\subset G_n$  到完整最小内点纤维的全局光滑嵌入。每个连通分支均具有精确维数

$$(n-1)^2.
\tag{27}$$

证明
经典最小实现唯一性已经保证同一可见律的两个最小核满足唯一的 reset-preserving 相似关系；该部分属于 Kalman 理论，Kalman 1963。本文所需的强化来自显式逆。
由  $L=M^{-1}KM$ 、 $M\mathbf1=\mathbf1$ ，

$$L^j\mathbf1=M^{-1}K^j\mathbf1,$$

故

$$C(L)=M^{-1}C(K).$$

最小性保证  $C(L)$  可逆，因而得到 (26)。该公式同时证明轨道参数的唯一性及逆映射的光滑性。严格正性条件正好将  $G_n$  截取为  $\Omega_K$ ，故没有额外的不可见分支。证毕。
进一步，尾生成函数

$$H_K(t)=\sum_{j\ge0}\beta K^j\mathbf1\,t^j
      =\beta(I-tK)^{-1}\mathbf1
\tag{28}$$

是完整可见律的有限维有理坐标。在一般  $\det K\neq0$  的最小层上，其归一化分子—分母具有  $2n-1$  个自由系数，而

$$n^2-(n-1)^2=2n-1.
\tag{29}$$

这表明  $(n-1)^2$  维相似轨道恰好耗尽全部结构性不可识别方向；其余  $2n-1$  个方向构成可见有理模空间。任何基于单一可见记录的似然或估计方程，其隐藏核 Fisher 信息矩阵至少具有  $(n-1)^2$  维零空间。
发表价值判断
结果建议定位价值判断精确物理像方程 (1) 与跨对角线检验 (3)–(4)新主定理最高；将“可逆”提升为“可检验的像刻画” $N^{-1/4}$  根分裂及原子—连续混合极限新主定理最高；显著超出论文现有 Theorem E割线谱公式、尖锐界及 Lambert  $W$  相界新主定理或强推论组高；给出完整相关相图与普适衰减界舍入表示及半区间采样偏差独立命题/应用定理中高；对采样设计具有直接解释力全局主轨道与  $2n-1$  维可见模空间高维结构定理高；将局部“存在”强化为完整纤维分类
其中，定理 1 与定理 3 已足以构成一条独立且具有发表价值的新理论主线：前者刻画正则的物理模型像，后者刻画同一模型像边界上不可避免的二阶识别奇异性。二者共同揭示了一个此前未被论文识别的核心事实——交换对角线对“模型归属”是正则的，而对“根分裂”却是  $N^{-1/4}$  奇异的。

One immediate point requiring scrutiny is the proposed 1-dependence criterion: on the diagonal \(x=y\), the equation \(xe^{-x}=ye^{-y}\) is tautological, whereas the continuous extension of the hidden eigenvalue vanishes only at \(x=y=1\); I am checking whether the manuscript already excludes that diagonal or whether this is a genuine counterexample to the claim as stated.

   130: 
   131: \noindent\textbf{Keywords:} constrained D-MAP, phase-type identifiability,
   132: similarity orbit, confluent Prony method, renewal process, exact
   133: \(1\)-dependence
   134: 
   135: \section{Introduction}
   136: 
   137: This paper is about a constrained two-state discrete-time Markovian-arrival
   138: process (D-MAP) and the stationary binary renewal record it generates.  The
   139: physical input is deliberately modest: a latched killed-leakage sampled
   140: counter with two
   141: internal states, \(R\) for ready and \(D\) for dead/recovering.  During one
   142: sampling interval of length \(\Delta\tau\), a ready counter attempts to click
   143: with exponential rate \(\Gamma\), and a dead counter recovers with exponential
   144: rate \(\kappa_{\mathrm r}\).  A click is recorded as \(A_n=1\), and the
   145: boundary-reset convention assigns the post-click state at the next sample to
   146: \(D\).  This construction derives the labelled kernel pair displayed in
   147: \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}: \(T_0+T_1\) is the hidden transition
   148: matrix, \(T_1\) marks an observed \(1\), and the killed entries
   149: \(T_1(R,R)=T_1(D,R)=0\) encode the rule that every recorded click leaves the
   150: counter dead at the next boundary.  No theorem below allows either
   151: one-labelled entry into \(R\) to be positive; such mass is same-bin reset
   152: leakage and belongs to a different labelled kernel model.  Thus
   153: \((\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r},\Delta\tau)\) are not formal decoration; they are
   154: the attempt rate, recovery rate, and sampling interval of this sampled-counter
   155: realization of the D-MAP.
   156: 
   157: The paper-level contribution is the closed-form record law, quotient inverse,
   158: and fixed-setting retained-record asymptotic analysis of this constrained
   159: D-MAP(2) subfamily, together with an \(n\)-state identifiability theorem that
   160: separates pure serial models from general killed-reset realizations by their
   161: Markovian similarity-orbit fibres. The following contract fixes the physical
   162: model used by the two-state record and inference statements.
   163: 
   164: This positioning is narrower than several nearby literatures.  The closest
   165: probability comparator is Zamparo's stationary renewal-binary framework
   166: \cite{Zamparo2022RenewalBinary}: it supplies a renewal-binary language and
   167: Gaussian limit technology, but it does not derive the renewal law from a
   168: triangular killed-leakage sampled-counter D-MAP, compute the Palm gap law and
   169: monotone hazard in \((\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r},\Delta\tau)\), or invert three
   170: visible inclusion coordinates to \((p+s,ps)\).  Classical MAP and D-MAP theory
   171: \cite{Neuts1979MAP,Lucantoni1991BMAP,AlfaNeuts1995DMAP} supplies the
   172: arrival-process background, while MAP2 and phase-type nonidentifiability
   173: \cite{RamirezCoboLilloWiper2010MAP2Nonidentifiability,
   174: OCinneide1989PhaseTypeNonuniqueness} explain why our inverse is deliberately a
   175: visible quotient inverse rather than a labelled hidden-kernel reconstruction.
   176: 
   177: Recent physics-math work on weak memory, waiting times, correlations, and
   178: partial observations is close in motivation but not in theorem object.  Weak
   179: memory and Markov-state diagnostics
   180: \cite{Brandner2025WeakMemoryPRE,MeyerBrandner2026WeakMemory,
   181: HartichGodec2021EmergentMemory,VollmarEtAl2024Memory,
   182: ZhaoMakarovGodec2025MarkovStateHolography,LoosGodec2025NonMarkovianEffects}
   183: study memory strength, response, or adequacy of coarse dynamical models; here
   184: the record theorem gives the exact one-mode covariance tail
   185: \(\gamma_n=\rho(a-\rho)\lambda_{\rm hid}^{\,n-1}\), the exact
   186: \(1\)-dependence threshold, and a visible quotient inverse.  Transition
   187: waiting-time and partial-observation inference
   188: \cite{VanDerMeerErtelSeifert2022TransitionWTD,
   189: HarunariDuttaPolettiniRoldan2022VisibleTransitions,
   190: ErtelSeifert2024BlurredTransitions,BauerSeifertVanderMeer2025Stroboscopic,
   191: MaierSeifertVanderMeer2025HiddenPaths} targets generators, entropy production,
   192: hidden paths, or transition-waiting-time functionals under partial access.  The
   193: waiting-time object here is instead the Palm zero-run gap law \(g_k\) of the
   194: binary click record, used to build the complete visible renewal law and the
   195: symmetric sampled-counter quotient.
   196: 
   197: Detector dead-time and photon-counting models are the closest instrument-level
   198: comparators
   199: \cite{Muller1973,CantorTeich1975,YuFessler2000,Hadfield2009,
   200: EisamanEtAl2011,StrakaEtAl2020SPADCounting,KirchhoffEtAl2025Deadtime,
   201: JorgensenJohnson2026DED}.  They address count corrections, device recovery,
   202: afterpulsing, finite-window distributions, or detector-specific estimators.
   203: The theorem chain below instead fixes the boundary-reset killed-leakage kernel
   204: and asks exactly what its stationary binary record identifies.  The structural
   205: fragility of this convention is recorded in
   206: \Cref{prop:model-fragility-boundary-reset-main}: allowing one-labelled
   207: same-bin recovery into \(R\) destroys the deterministic post-click reset on
   208: which the renewal product, quotient inverse, and retained-record covariance
   209: route depend.
   210: 
   211: \begin{assumption}[Constrained sampled-counter D-MAP model]\label{ass:model-inference-contract-main}
   212: Fix \((\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r},\Delta\tau)\in(0,\infty)^3\) and define
   213: \[
   214: p=e^{-\Gamma\Delta\tau},\qquad
   215: s=e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau},
   216: \]
   217: \[
   218: a=\int_0^{\Delta\tau}\kappa_{\mathrm r}e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}r}
   219: \bigl(1-e^{-\Gamma(\Delta\tau-r)}\bigr)\,dr,\qquad
   220: b=\int_0^{\Delta\tau}\kappa_{\mathrm r}e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}r}
   221: e^{-\Gamma(\Delta\tau-r)}\,dr .
   222: \]
   223: The record model is the two-state D-MAP with hidden states ordered as
   224: \((R,D)\) and labelled kernels
   225: \[
   226: T_0=\begin{pmatrix}p&0\\ b&s\end{pmatrix},
   227: \qquad
   228: T_1=\begin{pmatrix}0&1-p\\0&a\end{pmatrix}.
   229: \]
   230: The convention is
   231: \((T_i)_{XY}=\PP(A_n=i,H_{n+1}=Y\mid H_n=X)\), the visible process
   232: \((A_n)_{n\in\mathbb Z}\) is the stationary binary process generated by these
   233: kernels, and formulas on \(\Gamma=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\) are interpreted by
   234: continuous limits.  The killed entries \(T_1(R,R)=T_1(D,R)=0\) are a structural
   235: constraint of this D-MAP, not optional boundary cases of the finite-record
   236: theorems.
   237: \end{assumption}
   238: 
   239: \subsection{Scope of finite-record reports}\label{subsec:finite-record-report-scope-main}

  1081: \iffalse % Detailed restatement moved to the supplementary source.
  1082: \begin{proposition}[Detailed form of Theorem~A: constrained D-MAP record law]\label{prop:main-record-wrapper-detailed}
  1083: Let \((A_n)_{n\in\mathbb Z}\) have law
  1084: \(\mu_{\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r},\Delta\tau}\) from
  1085: \Cref{def:main-sampled-kernel-law}.  Let \(G\) be the
  1086: number of zeros between consecutive ones, and put
  1087: \[
  1088: \rho=\PP(A_0=1),\qquad
  1089: \lambda_{\mathrm{hid}}=p-b .
  1090: \]
  1091: Then \((A_n)\) is a full-support renewal process with Palm gap law
  1092: \[
  1093: g_k=\PP(G=k)=e_D^\top T_0^kT_1\mathbf1,\qquad k\ge0 .
  1094: \]
  1095: If \(\Gamma\ne\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), then
  1096: \[
  1097: g_k=
  1098: \frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}(1-p)p^k-\Gamma(1-s)s^k}
  1099:      {\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
  1100: \]
  1101: while on the equal-rate diagonal the formula is interpreted by the continuous
  1102: limit, equivalently for
  1103: \(y=\Gamma\Delta\tau=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\) and \(p=e^{-y}\),
  1104: \[
  1105: g_k=p^k\bigl((1-p)(1+yk)-yp\bigr).
  1106: \]
  1107: Writing
  1108: \[
  1109: S_k=\PP(G\ge k),\qquad h_k=\PP(G=k\mid G\ge k)=g_k/S_k ,
  1110: \]
  1111: the survival tail, hazard, and hazard increment are explicit.  Off the
  1112: equal-rate diagonal,
  1113: \[
  1114: S_k=
  1115: \frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^k-\Gamma s^k}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
  1116: \qquad
  1117: h_k=
  1118: 1-\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^{k+1}-\Gamma s^{k+1}}
  1119:         {\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^k-\Gamma s^k},
  1120: \]
  1121: and
  1122: \[
  1123: h_{k+1}-h_k
  1124: =
  1125: \frac{
  1126:   \Gamma\kappa_{\mathrm r}\,
  1127:   (p-s)^2\,p^k s^k
  1128: }{
  1129:   \bigl(\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^k-\Gamma s^k\bigr)
  1130:   \bigl(\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^{k+1}-\Gamma s^{k+1}\bigr)
  1131: }>0 .
  1132: \]
  1133: On the equal-rate diagonal,
  1134: \[
  1135: S_k=p^k(1+yk),\qquad
  1136: h_k=1-p-\frac{yp}{1+yk},
  1137: \qquad
  1138: h_{k+1}-h_k=
  1139: \frac{y^2p}{(1+yk)(1+y(k+1))}>0 .
  1140: \]
  1141: Thus the zero-run hazard is strictly increasing.  For every \(n\ge1\),
  1142: \[
  1143: \gamma_n:=\operatorname{Cov}(A_0,A_n)
  1144: =\rho(a-\rho)\lambda_{\mathrm{hid}}^{\,n-1}.
  1145: \]
  1146: The record is exact \(1\)-dependent iff \(\lambda_{\mathrm{hid}}=0\), and in no
  1147: positive parameter case is it a finite-order Markov chain.
  1148: \end{proposition}

  1154: \section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
  1155: 
  1156: The paper gives a closed-form record-law and fixed-setting asymptotic analysis
  1157: for one constrained latched killed-leakage sampled-counter D-MAP(2) subfamily.
  1158: The visible
  1159: record identifies the unordered survival/rate coordinates, not hidden labels
  1160: or response-side thermodynamic parameters without external calibration.  The

 succeeded in 480ms:
   416: \subsection{Consolidated main theorem block}\label{subsec:main-theorem-boundary}
   417: 
   418: \begin{paperthm}[Constrained D-MAP record law]\label{thm:main-record-wrapper}
   419: Let \((A_n)_{n\in\mathbb Z}\) have the stationary visible law generated by the
   420: sampled-counter kernels in \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}.  Put
   421: \[
   422: p=e^{-\Gamma\Delta\tau},\qquad
   423: s=e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau},\qquad
   424: \rho=\PP(A_0=1),\qquad
   425: \lambda_{\rm hid}=p-b .
   426: \]
   427: Then \((A_n)\) is a full-support renewal process.  If \(G\) is the Palm number
   428: of zeros between consecutive ones, then
   429: \[
   430: g_k=\PP(G=k)=e_D^\top T_0^kT_1{\bf 1},\qquad k\ge0 .
   431: \]
   432: For \(\Gamma\ne\kappa_{\mathrm r}\),
   433: \[
   434: g_k=\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}(1-p)p^k-\Gamma(1-s)s^k}
   435:           {\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
   436: \qquad
   437: S_k=\PP(G\ge k)=
   438: \frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^k-\Gamma s^k}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
   439: \]
   440: with continuous-limit formulas on \(\Gamma=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\).  The zero-run
   441: hazard \(h_k=g_k/S_k\) is strictly increasing.  The visible covariance has the
   442: single hidden-mode tail
   443: \[
   444: \operatorname{Cov}(A_0,A_n)=\rho(a-\rho)\lambda_{\rm hid}^{\,n-1},
   445: \qquad n\ge1 .
   446: \]
   447:   The record is exact \(1\)-dependent if and only if
   448:   \(\lambda_{\rm hid}=0\).  Moreover
   449:   \[
   450:   \gamma_1=\rho(a-\rho)<0,
   451:   \]
   452:   so adjacent outputs are not independent and the record is not
   453:   \(0\)-dependent.  In no positive parameter case is it a finite-order Markov
   454:   chain.
   455: \end{paperthm}
   456: 
   457: \begin{paperthm}[Quotient inverse]\label{thm:toeplitz_image_inverse_duality}
   458: Work in the same constrained D-MAP record model.  Let
   459: \[
   460: r_0=\rho,\qquad r_1=\rho a,\qquad r_2=\rho u_2,
   461: \]
   462: and assume the quotient inverse domain
   463: \[
   464: r_0>0,\qquad \Delta_{\rm inv}:=r_1-r_0^2\ne0 .
   465: \]
   466: Define
   467: \[
   468: a(r)=\frac{r_1}{r_0},\qquad
   469: \lambda(r)=\frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2},
   470: \]
   471: and
   472: \[
   473: \Phi(r)=
   474: \left(1-a(r)+\lambda(r),\,
   475: r_0(1-\lambda(r))-a(r)+\lambda(r)\right).
   476: \]
   477: On the constrained D-MAP image,
   478: \[
   479: \Phi(r)=(p+s,ps).
   480: \]
   481: Thus the visible law determines the unordered survival-factor pair
   482: \(\{p,s\}\), equivalently the unordered roots of
   483: \[
   484: z^2-\Phi_1(r)z+\Phi_2(r)=0,
   485: \]
   486: and determines all visible renewal probabilities through this quotient.  For
   487: known design interval \(\Delta\tau\), the same quotient determines the
   488: unordered numerical rate set
   489: \[
   490: \left\{-\frac{\log p}{\Delta\tau},-\frac{\log s}{\Delta\tau}\right\}
   491: =\{\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\}.
   492: \]
   493: If \(\Delta\tau\) is not supplied as a known design interval, the numerical
   494: rate display is replaced by the dimensionless identifiable set
   495: \[
   496: \{-\log p,-\log s\}
   497: =\{\Gamma\Delta\tau,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\}.
   498: \]
   499: In either case the visible law does not determine an ordered labelled vector
   500: \((\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r})\) unless external side information fixes the
   501: physical assignment.  On the exchange diagonal, the
   502: invariant visible-law estimand is the common dimensionless product, and only
   503: for known \(\Delta\tau\) the common numerical rate, not an ordered
   504: attempt/recovery label.
   505: \end{paperthm}
   506: 
   507: \begin{proposition}[Uniformly stable quotient chart]\label{prop:stable-quotient-chart-main}
   508: Put \(x=\Gamma\Delta\tau\), \(y=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\), and, for
   509: \(0<\varepsilon\le M<\infty\), restrict the dimensionless parameters to
   510: \[
   511: \Theta_{\varepsilon,M}=\{(x,y):\varepsilon\le x,y\le M\}.
   512: \]
   513: Writing \(q=1-e^{-x}\) and \(q_{\min}=1-e^{-\varepsilon}\), every point in
   514: this chart satisfies
   515: \[
   516: r_0\ge \frac{q_{\min}^2}{2},\qquad
   517: |\Delta_{\rm inv}|
   518: \ge \frac{\varepsilon e^{-2M}q_{\min}^3}{4}.
   519: \]
   520: Consequently \(D\Phi\) is uniformly bounded on
   521: \(\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}\).  If in addition \(|p-s|\ge\delta>0\), the sorted
   522: root Jacobian is bounded by a constant of order \(\delta^{-1}\), while
   523: \(p,s\ge e^{-M}\) uniformly bounds the log-rate derivative.  Thus
   524: \(\Theta_{\varepsilon,M,\delta}:=\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}\cap
   525: \{|p-s|\ge\delta\}\) is a uniformly stable separated-root/rate chart; the
   526: exchange diagonal is excluded and remains nonregular.
   527: \end{proposition}
   528: 
   529: \begin{proof}
   530: The integral representation of \(b\) gives
   531: \[
   532: b=y\int_0^1e^{-yt-x(1-t)}\,dt\ge \varepsilon e^{-M}.
   533: \]
   534: Moreover \(1-s\ge q_{\min}\), \(a\le q(1-s)\), and hence
   535: \(q-a\ge qs\ge q_{\min}e^{-M}\).  From
   536: \[
   537: \rho=\frac{q(1-s)}{q+b},\qquad
   538: \rho-a=\frac{b(q-a)}{q+b},
   539: \]
   540: and \(q+b\le2\),
   541: \[
   542: r_0=\rho\ge\frac{q_{\min}^2}{2},\qquad
   543: |\Delta_{\rm inv}|=\rho(\rho-a)
   544: =\frac{q(1-s)b(q-a)}{(q+b)^2}
   545: \ge\frac{\varepsilon e^{-2M}q_{\min}^3}{4}.
   546: \]
   547: All entries of the rational matrix \(D\Phi\) are therefore uniformly bounded
   548: on the compact chart.  The root derivative contains only the additional
   549: denominator \(z_+-z_-=|p-s|\), and the derivative of
   550: \(-\log z/\Delta\tau\) is \(-(\Delta\tau z)^{-1}\); the last two assertions
   551: follow from \(|p-s|\ge\delta\) and \(p,s\ge e^{-M}\).
   552: \end{proof}
   553: 
   554: \begin{lemma}[Specialized separated covariance estimator for adjacent gap rewards]\label{lem:paper-level-separated-covariance-main}
   555: Let \(G_j\) be i.i.d. nonnegative integer-valued variables with
   556: \(\EE e^{\eta G_0}<\infty\) for some \(\eta>0\).  Put \(L_j=G_j+1\),
   557: \(\mu=\EE L_0\), \(K_N=\max\{k:\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}L_j\le N\}\), and
   558: \(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\in\RR^d\), where \(\EE Y_j=0\) and
   559: \(\EE\|Y_j\|^4<\infty\).  Set
   560: \[
   561: \Sigma_r=\frac{1}{\mu}\{\operatorname{Var}Y_0+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
   562: +\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)\}.
   563: \]
   564: With \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), \(d_N=b_N+1\),
   565: \[
   566: I_{\ell,N}=\{(\ell-1)d_N,\ldots,(\ell-1)d_N+b_N-1\},
   567: \qquad
   568: M_N=\max\{M:(M-1)d_N+b_N\le K_N-1\},
   569: \]
   570: let \(B_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}Y_j\).  On \(M_N\ge2\), put
   571: \(\bar B_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}B_{\ell,N}\) and
   572: \[
   573: \widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
   574: =\frac{K_N}{N}\frac{1}{b_N(M_N-1)}
   575: \sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)^\top .
   576: \]
   577: Then \(M_N\ge2\) with probability tending to one, and the auxiliary zero
   578: completion on \(M_N<2\) satisfies
   579: \[
   580: \|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3}).
   581: \]
   582: If \(\widehat Y_j=Y_j-\Delta_NL_j\) with
   583: \(\Delta_N=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), the same rate holds for the corresponding
   584: plug-in separated-batch estimator.
   585: For any deterministic \(m_N\to\infty\) with
   586: \(m_N=o(N/b_N)\), the operational reporting gate is strengthened to
   587: \(M_N\ge m_N\); this event also has probability tending to one.  The condition
   588: \(M_N\ge2\) is used only to define the auxiliary estimator on all samples.
   589: 
   590: \begin{proof}
   591: The variables \(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\) are centered and \(1\)-dependent.  Since
   592: the deterministic blocks \(I_{\ell,N}\) are separated by one deleted cycle,
   593: the block sums \(B_{\ell,N}\) are independent for deterministic prefixes.
   594: Writing
   595: \[
   596: T_1=\operatorname{Var}Y_0+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
   597: +\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0),
   598: \]
   599: one has \(b_N^{-1}\operatorname{Var}B_{\ell,N}=T_1+O(b_N^{-1})\).  The
   600: exponential moment of \(G_0\) gives uniform fourth moments for the block sums.
   601: Define the independent centered matrices
   602: \[
   603: X_{\ell,N}=b_N^{-1}\{B_{\ell,N}B_{\ell,N}^{\top}
   604: -\EE(B_{\ell,N}B_{\ell,N}^{\top})\},
   605: \qquad \sup_{\ell,N}\EE\|X_{\ell,N}\|_F^2\le C_X.
   606: \]
   607: For fixed \(0<c<C<\infty\), on
   608: \(cN/b_N\le M_N\le CN/b_N\), Kolmogorov's maximal inequality applied
   609: entrywise, followed by a union bound over the \(d^2\) entries, gives
   610: \[
   611: \PP\!\left\{
   612: \max_{m\le CN/b_N}\left\|\sum_{\ell=1}^mX_{\ell,N}\right\|_F
   613: >\eta N/b_N\right\}
   614: \le \frac{C_d b_N}{\eta^2N}.
   615: \]
   616: At the natural \(\sqrt{N/b_N}\) scale the same inequality yields
   617: \[
   618: \left\|\frac1{M_N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}X_{\ell,N}\right\|_{\rm op}
   619: =O_{\PP}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
   620: \]
   621: The empirical block mean contributes \(O_{\PP}(b_N/N)\).  The renewal count
   622: obeys
   623: \[
   624: K_N=N/\mu+O_{\PP}(\sqrt N),
   625: \]
   626: so the stopped block count is controlled by
   627: \[
   628: M_N=\frac{N}{\mu d_N}+O_{\PP}(\sqrt N/b_N+1),
   629: \]
   630: equivalently
   631: \[
   632: M_N=\frac{N}{\mu b_N}
   633:   +O_{\PP}(N/b_N^2+\sqrt N/b_N+1),
   634: \]
   635: where the deterministic \(N/b_N^2\) term is only the one-cycle separator
   636: correction, and \(b_NM_N/K_N=1+O_{\PP}(b_N^{-1})\).  Combining these estimates
   637: with \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\) proves the stopped-prefix replacement.  For the plug-in
   638: rewards, the exponential moment of \(L_j\) supplies constants \(c_L,C_0>0\),
   639: with \(C_0\) sufficiently large, such that the Chernoff and union bounds give
   640: \[
   641: \PP\!\left\{
   642: \max_{\ell\le CN/b_N}\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}L_j>C_0b_N
   643: \right\}
   644: \le \frac{CN}{b_N}e^{-c_Lb_N}=o(1).
   645: \]
   646: Consequently
   647: \[
   648: \max_{\ell\le M_N}\left\|\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}(\widehat Y_j-Y_j)\right\|
   649: \le \|\Delta_N\|\max_{\ell\le M_N}\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}L_j
   650: =O_{\PP}(b_NN^{-1/2}),
   651: \]
   652: By Cauchy--Schwarz, the covariance cross term is
   653: \(O_{\PP}(\sqrt{b_N/N})\); the quadratic perturbation is
   654: \(O_{\PP}(b_N/N)\).  Combining these terms with the block bias gives
   655: \[
   656: \|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}-\Sigma_r\|_{\rm op}
   657: =O_{\PP}\!\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
   658: \]
   659: The choice \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\) proves the stated
   660: \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3})\) rate.
   661: \end{proof}
   662: \end{lemma}
   663: 
   664: \begin{paperthm}[Fixed-setting retained-record inclusion CLT]\label{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main}
   665: Assume the stationary model in \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main} and
   666: retain only cycles fully observed within the length-\(N\) record.  Form the
   667: complete-cycle inclusion estimator
   668: \[
   669: \Rinch_N^{\rm cyc}
   670: =\left(\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{0,N}}U_j^{(0)},
   671:        \frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{1,N}}U_j^{(1)},
   672:        \frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{2,N}}U_j^{(2)}\right)^\top ,
   673: \qquad
   674: \Rinc=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top .
   675: \]
   676: In Palm cycle coordinates put
   677: \[
   678: U_j=
   679: \bigl(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
   680: \mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\bigr)^\top,
   681: \qquad
   682: L_j=G_j+1,\qquad
   683: Y_j=U_j-\Rinc L_j .
   684: \]
   685: Then \(\EE Y_j=0\), \((Y_j)\) is \(1\)-dependent, and
   686: \[
   687: \sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\rm cyc}-\Rinc)
   688: =\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j+o_{\PP}(1)
   689: \Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r),
   690: \]
   691: where
   692: \[
   693: \Sigma_r=\frac1\mu\{\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)
   694: +\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)\},
   695: \qquad \mu=\EE L_0 .
   696: \]
   697: The separated-batch covariance estimator with
   698: \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), plug-in centering, and the operational gate
   699: \(M_N\ge m_N\), where \(m_N\to\infty\) and \(m_N=o(N/b_N)\), obeys
   700: \[
   701: \|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\rm op}=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3})
   702: \]
   703: on an event whose probability tends to one.
   704: \end{paperthm}
   705: 
   706: \begin{paperthm}[Fixed-setting retained-record quotient delta method]\label{thm:finite-record-quotient-delta-main}
   707: Assume the hypotheses of Theorem~C and suppose the population inverse lies in
   708: the stable quotient chart
   709: \[
   710: r_0>0,\qquad \Delta_{\rm inv}=r_1-r_0^2\ne0 .
   711: \]
   712: For the quotient map \(\Phi\) in Theorem~B,
   713: \[
   714: \sqrt N\{\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\rm cyc})-\Phi(\Rinc)\}
   715: \Rightarrow
   716: \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma),
   717: \qquad
   718: \Sigma_\sigma=D\Phi(\Rinc)\Sigma_rD\Phi(\Rinc)^\top .
   719: \]
   720: For the constrained D-MAP population, \(\Phi(\Rinc)=(p+s,ps)\).  If
   721: \[
   722: \Delta_\sigma=\Phi_1(\Rinc)^2-4\Phi_2(\Rinc)=(p-s)^2>0,
   723: \]
   724: let \(z_-<z_+\) be the sorted roots and let
   725: \(\widehat z_{-,N}<\widehat z_{+,N}\) be the correspondingly sorted roots
   726: formed from \(\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\rm cyc})\).  This ordering is visible and
   727: requires no external physical label.  With
   728: \[
   729: J_{\rm root}=
   730: \begin{pmatrix}
   731: \dfrac{z_-}{z_--z_+}&-\dfrac1{z_--z_+}\\[6pt]
   732: \dfrac{z_+}{z_+-z_-}&-\dfrac1{z_+-z_-}
   733: \end{pmatrix},
   734: \]
   735: the label-free root CLT is
   736: \[
   737: \sqrt N
   738: \begin{pmatrix}\widehat z_{-,N}-z_-\\ \widehat z_{+,N}-z_+\end{pmatrix}
   739: \Rightarrow
   740: \mathcal N\!\left(0,J_{\rm root}\Sigma_\sigma J_{\rm root}^{\top}\right).
   741: \]
   742: For known design interval \(\Delta\tau\), define the root-ordered rates
   743: \[
   744: \vartheta=
   745: \begin{pmatrix}-\log z_-/\Delta\tau\\-\log z_+/\Delta\tau\end{pmatrix},
   746: \qquad
   747: \widehat\vartheta_N=
   748: \begin{pmatrix}-\log\widehat z_{-,N}/\Delta\tau\\
   749: -\log\widehat z_{+,N}/\Delta\tau\end{pmatrix}.
   750: \]
   751: Then
   752: \[
   753: \sqrt N(\widehat\vartheta_N-\vartheta)
   754: \Rightarrow\mathcal N(0,J_{\rm rate}\Sigma_\sigma J_{\rm rate}^{\top}),
   755: \qquad
   756: J_{\rm rate}=
   757: \begin{pmatrix}-(\Delta\tau z_-)^{-1}&0\\
   758: 0&-(\Delta\tau z_+)^{-1}\end{pmatrix}J_{\rm root}.
   759: \]
   760: Thus the roots identify the unordered survival-factor set \(\{p,s\}\) and
   761: the displayed root ordering gives a canonical, label-free version of the
   762: unordered numerical rate set
   763: \[
   764: \left\{-\frac{\log p}{\Delta\tau},-\frac{\log s}{\Delta\tau}\right\}.
   765: \]
   766: If \(\Delta\tau\) is not supplied as a known design interval, the theorem
   767: identifies only the dimensionless unordered product set
   768: \[
   769: \{-\log p,-\log s\}
   770: =\{\Gamma\Delta\tau,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\},
   771: \]
   772: not numerical rates per unit time.
   773: Only the assignment of the two numerical entries to the physical names
   774: \(\Gamma\) and \(\kappa_{\mathrm r}\) requires the side-data branch
   775: convention.
   776: \end{paperthm}
   777: 
   778: \begin{paperthm}[Pointwise exchange-diagonal projection example]\label{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}
   779: Fix
   780: \[
   781: z_0=\frac12,\qquad \sigma_0=(1,1/4),\qquad
   782: H_{z_0}=\{h\in\mathbb R^2:h_2\le z_0h_1\},
   783: \]
   784: and let \(F=\{(\sigma_1,\sigma_2):\sigma_2\le\sigma_1^2/4\}\).  Suppose the
   785: true population satisfies \(\sigma=\sigma_0\), the printed interval
   786: certificate proves
   787: \(\lambda_{\min}\{\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\}>0.037\), and
   788: \[
   789: \sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}-\sigma_0)
   790: \Rightarrow Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)).
   791: \]
   792: Let \(\Pi_F\) be the locally unique Euclidean projection, with any fixed
   793: measurable tie rule outside the local neighbourhood, and set
   794: \[
   795: \widehat\sigma_N=\Pi_F(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}),\qquad
   796: X_N=\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_0).
   797: \]
   798: Then
   799: \[
   800: X_N\Rightarrow \Pi_{H_{z_0}}Z .
   801: \]
   802: This theorem is only a pointwise projection example at the preselected point
   803: \(z_0=1/2\).  It defines no statistic for testing, no simulated or limiting
   804: critical value, no confidence region, and no feasible or oracle coverage
   805: claim.  Selection of \(z_0\) from the data and uniformity over \(0<z<1\) are
   806: outside its scope.
   807: \end{paperthm}
   808: 
   809: \begin{proof}
   810: Near \(\sigma_0\), the boundary of \(F\) is the smooth graph
   811: \(\sigma_2=\sigma_1^2/4\), whose tangent cone at \(\sigma_0\) is
   812: \(H_{z_0}\).  The standard first-order metric-projection expansion at a smooth
   813: inequality boundary is
   814: \[
   815: \sqrt N\{\Pi_F(\sigma_0+N^{-1/2}h_N)-\sigma_0\}
   816: =\Pi_{H_{z_0}}h_N+o(1)
   817: \]
   818: for every bounded convergent sequence \(h_N\)
   819: \cite{Shapiro1987MetricProjection}.  Tightness of the raw quotient CLT permits
   820: the expansion on events with probability tending to one.  Slutsky's theorem
   821: and continuity of projection onto the closed half-space give the asserted
   822: limit.  The certificate establishes only that the covariance in this limit is
   823: nonsingular; it is not used to calibrate a procedure.
   824: \end{proof}
   825: 
   826: \subsection{Proofs of Theorems A--D}\label{subsec:proofs-a-d-main}
   827: 
   828: \begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~A]
   829: Write \(\beta=e_D^\top\) and
   830: \(c=((1-p),a)^\top\).  The marked kernel has rank-one reset form
   831: \(T_1=c\beta\).  Conditional on every click, the next hidden state is therefore
   832: \(D\), independently of the pre-click state.  Successive click epochs are
   833: regeneration epochs, and the numbers of intervening zeros are independent with
   834: common Palm mass
   835: \[
   836: g_k=\beta T_0^kT_1\mathbf1,\qquad k\ge0.
   837: \]
   838: The entries of \(T_0\) are strictly positive along every admissible serial path,
   839: so \(g_k>0\) for all \(k\); the spectral radius of \(T_0\) is less than one,
   840: and \(\sum_{k\ge0}g_k=1\).  This proves the full-support renewal assertion.
   841: 
   842: Since \(T_0\) is lower triangular, direct evaluation of \(T_0^k\), followed by
   843: substitution of the integral defining \(b\), gives, off the equal-rate diagonal,
   844: \[
   845: g_k=\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}(1-p)p^k-
   846: \Gamma(1-s)s^k}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},\qquad
   847: S_k=\sum_{j\ge k}g_j=
   848: \frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^k-\Gamma s^k}
   849: {\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma}.
   850: \]
   851: The continuous limit at \(\Gamma=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\) follows either by
   852: l'H\^opital's rule or by exponentiating the repeated-eigenvalue triangular
   853: matrix.  Because \(h_k=1-S_{k+1}/S_k\), elementary cancellation yields
   854: \[
   855: h_{k+1}-h_k=
   856: \frac{\Gamma\kappa_{\mathrm r}(p-s)^2p^ks^k}
   857: {(\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^k-\Gamma s^k)
   858:  (\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^{k+1}-\Gamma s^{k+1})}>0.
   859: \]
   860: On the diagonal, with \(y=\Gamma\Delta\tau\) and \(p=e^{-y}\), the same
   861: calculation gives
   862: \[
   863: S_k=p^k(1+yk),\qquad
   864: h_{k+1}-h_k=\frac{y^2p}{(1+yk)(1+y(k+1))}>0.
   865: \]
   866: 
   867: Let \(P=T_0+T_1\) be the hidden transition matrix.  Its eigenvalues are
   868: \(1\) and \(\lambda_{\rm hid}=p-b\).  Conditional on a click at time zero,
   869: the state at time one is \(D\), and hence
   870: \[
   871: u_n:=\PP(A_n=1\mid A_0=1)
   872: =\rho+(a-\rho)\lambda_{\rm hid}^{n-1},\qquad n\ge1.
   873: \]
   874: Multiplication by \(\rho\) gives the covariance formula.  With
   875: \(q=1-p\), stationarity gives
   876: \[
   877: \rho=\frac{q(1-s)}{q+b},\qquad
   878: \rho-a=\frac{b(q-a)}{q+b}>0,
   879: \]
   880: so \(\gamma_1=\rho(a-\rho)<0\) and \(0\)-dependence is excluded.  If
   881: \(\lambda_{\rm hid}=0\), the two rows of \(P\) coincide; the hidden state after
   882: one intervening step has forgotten the past, which makes visible blocks
   883: separated by one omitted observation independent.  Conversely, exact
   884: \(1\)-dependence implies \(\operatorname{Cov}(A_0,A_2)=0\), and the strict
   885: prefactor in the covariance formula forces \(\lambda_{\rm hid}=0\).
   886: Finally, in a binary renewal process an order-\(m\) Markov property would make
   887: the click hazard constant after every zero run of length at least \(m\).  The
   888: strictly increasing hazards above rule this out for every finite \(m\).
   889: \end{proof}
   890: 
   891: \begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~B]
   892: The regenerative reset gives
   893: \[
   894: r_0=\rho,\qquad
   895: r_1=\rho\PP(A_1=1\mid A_0=1)=\rho a,
   896: \qquad r_2=\rho u_2.
   897: \]
   898: The spectral calculation in the proof of Theorem~A gives
   899: \(u_2=\rho+(a-\rho)\lambda_{\rm hid}\).  Therefore, on
   900: \(r_1-r_0^2\ne0\),
   901: \[
   902: a=\frac{r_1}{r_0},qquad
   903: \lambda_{\rm hid}=
   904: \frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2}.
   905: \]
   906: Since \(a=1-s-b\) and \(\lambda_{\rm hid}=p-b\),
   907: \[
   908: 1-a+\lambda_{\rm hid}=p+s.
   909: \]
   910: Substitution of \(\rho=q(1-s)/(q+b)\), \(q=1-p\), into the second coordinate
   911: gives
   912: \[
   913: \rho(1-\lambda_{\rm hid})-a+\lambda_{\rm hid}=ps.
   914: \]
   915: These are precisely the two coordinates of \(\Phi\).  Their polynomial has
   916: root multiset \(\{p,s\}\).  That multiset determines the displayed Palm gap
   917: law, including its continuous repeated-root limit, and renewal products then
   918: determine every finite inclusion probability; finite inclusion--exclusion
   919: determines every word probability.  Thus the three coordinates are
   920: law-determining.  This argument does not select a physical label for either
   921: root and makes no minimal-cardinality claim.
   922: \end{proof}
   923: 
   924: \begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~C]
   925: Under Palm sampling, the gaps \((G_j)\) are i.i.d. with an exponential moment.
   926: The displayed cycle reward satisfies \(\EE U_j=\Rinc\EE L_j\), hence
   927: \(\EE Y_j=0\), and it depends only on \((G_j,G_{j+1})\), hence is
   928: \(1\)-dependent.  Complete-cycle deletion removes at most a fixed number of
   929: endpoint cycles; their total length is \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\) by the exponential
   930: moment.  The renewal count satisfies
   931: \(K_N=N/\mu+O_{\PP}(\sqrt N)\).  The random-index version of the
   932: Hoeffding--Robbins CLT for finite-range dependent variables therefore yields
   933: \[
   934: N^{-1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j
   935: \Rightarrow\mathcal N\!\left(0,
   936: \mu^{-1}\{\Var Y_0+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
   937: +\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)\}\right).
   938: \]
   939: The endpoint remainder is \(o_{\PP}(1)\) on this scale, proving the stated
   940: linearization and CLT.  Consistency and the rate of the stopped separated-batch
   941: covariance estimator are exactly the content of
   942: \Cref{lem:paper-level-separated-covariance-main}, including plug-in centering
   943: and the strengthened gate \(M_N\ge m_N\).
   944: \end{proof}
   945: 
   946: \begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~D]
   947: On the stable quotient chart, \(\Phi\) is continuously differentiable with the
   948: uniform bounds in \Cref{prop:stable-quotient-chart-main}.  Applying the
   949: multivariate delta method to Theorem~C gives the symmetric-coordinate CLT and
   950: its sandwich covariance.  When \(z_-<z_+\), implicit differentiation of
   951: \(z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2=0\) gives the displayed matrix \(J_{\rm root}\).
   952: The sorted roots remain in the same order with probability tending to one, so
   953: a second delta-method application gives the root CLT.  Composition with
   954: \(z\mapsto-\log z/\Delta\tau\) gives \(J_{\rm rate}\) and the root-ordered
   955: rate CLT.  No step assigns the two entries to physical labels.
   956: \end{proof}
   957: 
   958: The dependencies are linear: Theorem~A supplies the renewal law, Theorem~B the
   959: quotient inverse, Theorem~C the retained-record Gaussian input, Theorem~D the
   960: quotient delta method, and Theorem~E the local projection calculus at the

 succeeded in 289ms:
  1200: with the continuous equal-rate limit understood.  The contribution relative to
  1201: the detector dead-time literature is therefore not a generic dead-time
  1202: correction.  It is an exact quotient inverse for the sampled killed-leakage
  1203: D-MAP visible law, together with the renewal product and retained-record
  1204: covariance route that are valid only under the boundary-reset convention in
  1205: \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main,prop:model-fragility-boundary-reset-main}.
  1206: 
  1207: \subsection{Theorem-by-theorem comparison with DMAP, DPH, and MAP theory}
  1208: 
  1209: \paragraph{Theorem A (record law).}
  1210: Alfa--Neuts supply the general D-MAP construction
  1211: \cite{AlfaNeuts1995DMAP}, while M\'esz\'aros--Telek study canonical
  1212: representations for general discrete order-two MAPs and RAPs
  1213: \cite{MeszarosTelek2013DiscreteMAP2Canonical}.  Those representation results
  1214: do not assert that the present triangular, deterministically reset subfamily is
  1215: renewal.  The additional calculation here is restricted to that subfamily: it
  1216: derives the Palm gap law, increasing hazard, one-mode covariance, and exact
  1217: \(1\)-dependence criterion.  These are elementary model-specific consequences,
  1218: not a new general DMAP theorem.
  1219: 
  1220: \paragraph{Theorem B (three-inclusion quotient inverse).}
  1221: The closest prior result is the canonical-coordinate analysis of discrete
  1222: order-two MAP/RAP models by M\'esz\'aros--Telek
  1223: \cite{MeszarosTelek2013DiscreteMAP2Canonical}, together with canonical MAP2
  1224: and matrix-exponential representation work
  1225: \cite{BodrogHeindlHorvathTelek2008MAP2Canonical,
  1226: TelekHorvath2007MinimalMAP}.  That literature constructs sparse or canonical
  1227: representatives and moment-matching maps for broader representation classes.
  1228: Theorem~B neither replaces nor strengthens those canonical-form theorems.  Its
  1229: narrow additional statement is the direct rational identity
  1230: \[
  1231: \frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2}=p-b
  1232: \]
  1233: for the latched killed-reset image, which converts three visible binary
  1234: inclusion probabilities into \((p+s,ps)\) and hence the complete visible
  1235: renewal law.  The estimand is a law-determining quotient, not a labelled
  1236: minimal DMAP2 representative.  We do not claim that this identity is absent as
  1237: an implicit specialization of canonical DMAP2 coordinates; the contribution
  1238: is the explicit three-inclusion specialization and its stability bounds.
  1239: 
  1240: \paragraph{Theorems C and D (asymptotics).}
  1241: The regenerative CLT, separated-batch method, and multivariate delta method are
  1242: standard inputs \cite{Zamparo2022RenewalBinary,Janson2024RenewalCLT,
  1243: AsmussenGlynn2007,FlegalJones2010BatchMeansSpectral}.  The paper's additional
  1244: work is bookkeeping for complete retained cycles, the explicit stopped-prefix
  1245: bounds, and transport to the three-inclusion quotient.  The sorted-root and
  1246: log-rate CLTs are direct delta-method consequences and are not claimed as new
  1247: asymptotic theory.
  1248: 
  1249: \paragraph{Theorem E (projection example).}
  1250: Metric-projection limits at smooth inequality boundaries are standard
  1251: \cite{Shapiro1987MetricProjection,FangSeo2021ProjectionTests}.  Theorem~E is
  1252: therefore presented only as a pointwise worked projection at \(z_0=1/2\), with
  1253: a reproducible covariance nondegeneracy certificate.  It makes no inferential
  1254: or novelty claim beyond this specialization.
  1255: 
  1256: \paragraph{Higher-state results.}
  1257: Minimal-realization uniqueness is classical, beginning with Kalman and used
  1258: directly for MAP reduction and moment matching by Telek--Horv\'ath
  1259: \cite{Kalman1963LinearSystems,TelekHorvath2007MinimalMAP}.  Accordingly,
  1260: \Cref{thm:nstate-killed-reset-identifiability-dichotomy-main} is explicitly a
  1261: recalled orbit-fibre reformulation.  The new assertion is
  1262: \Cref{thm:nstate-interior-continuum-nonidentifiability-main}: the unrestricted
  1263: interior fibre contains a positivity-preserving similarity manifold of exact
  1264: dimension \((n-1)^2\).  For serial kernels, Cumani's canonical failure-time
  1265: forms, acyclic DPH estimation, and order-two/three discrete PH canonical forms
  1266: are direct predecessors
  1267: \cite{Cumani1982CanonicalFailure,BobbioEtAl2003AcyclicDPH,
  1268: PappTelek2013DiscretePHCanonical,MeszarosPappTelek2014Traffic}.
  1269: \Cref{cor:nstate-serial-all-collision-identifiability-main} is consequently
  1270: stated as a killed-reset orbit-intersection calculation: the pure serial fibre
  1271: is exactly the rate-multiset permutation class, with collisions handled by the
  1272: confluent recurrence.  It is not claimed as a new general acyclic-DPH
  1273: canonical-form theorem.  General phase-type nonuniqueness remains fully
  1274: consistent with O'Cinneide's invariant-polytope analysis
  1275: \cite{OCinneide1989PhaseTypeNonuniqueness,OCinneide1991InvariantPolytopes}.
  1276: 
  1277: The longer comparator tables and search ledger are moved to the supplementary
  1278: material.

    64: ## Novelty boundary
    65: 
    66: The minimal-realization similarity statement and the existence of multiple
    67: Markovian phase-type/MAP representatives are established theory and are cited
    68: as such. The manuscript does not claim to rediscover their proofs. The exact
    69: orbit--cone fibre criterion is a specialization of that theory to
    70: deterministic-reset D-MAPs and gives a sharp necessary-and-sufficient boundary
    71: for any declared subclass and structural quotient. The substantive
    72: model-specific conclusion is that
    73: the pure serial subclass is a transversal of those visible fibres at the level
    74: of the unordered rate multiset for every positive rate vector: repeated
    75: sampled poles retain their algebraic multiplicities through the confluent
    76: Hankel recurrence. Thus pole collision causes singular local coordinates and
    77: poor conditioning, but not loss of population identifiability. Coxian models
    78: with early absorption and general phase-type graphs remain outside this pure
    79: serial claim and can be nonunique, consistently with arXiv:1901.03849 and the
    80: classical literature.
    81: 
    82: No arXiv result returned by the stated searches asserts this exact
